Actually, wait. Let me reconsider the N-terminal region. Signal peptides are also hydrophobic but typically have a positively charged N-terminus, a hydrophobic core, and a cleavage site. This protein doesn&#x27;t seem to have a typical signal peptide (the N-terminus starts with MSFR which is not strongly positive).

Actually, I think the answer is A (Two). The protein seems to have two membrane-spanning regions:
1. An N-terminal region (positions ~1-18) that is moderately hydrophobic
2. A central region (positions ~87-105) that is strongly hydrophobic

For a 149 amino acid protein, having 2 TM segments is most reasonable. Three or more would be too many for the sequence length and the hydrophobic character of the available regions.

Let me double-check by looking at whether there could be a third TM region:
